The matching Third Dawn `serpents_snake_giant_pillage.grn` (SHA-256 `c1305092877edd08eb2e444847ed5a56f84dc94c86914ce418ac0cb4f343e21c`) was already decoded for Silver Serpent. Its 24 skeletal tracks matched the Giant Serpent by joint name, parent and rest transform. The bake copied their keys and recomputed only the floor-offset track for the Giant Serpentâ€™s own mesh. Five Forward+ frames rendered. Across 191 sampled poses per treatment, the largest complete-skin floor error was 2.02 mm and the native root stayed fixed. Existing animation keys and three static corpse hues passed. The isolated GiantSerpent constructor test passed 139 checks with both clients, including the original 2001â€“2018 hue range, slot 11, movement and persistent corpse replication. Activation awaits the running root verifier.

Reproduce with Godot `--headless --path Viewer --script res://../scripts/bake-giant-serpent-pillage.gd`, then `test-giant-serpent-pillage.gd`. Use `./scripts/test-horses.ps1 -Live -SkipContact -CreatureFamily giant_serpent_pillage` for native evidence and `python -B scripts/accept-classic-gestures.py --batch giant_serpent_pillage` to check the receipt.

The checked MUL decoder produced sheets for 19 source-backed gaps across the 15 highest-occurrence bodies in the tracked queue, using configured Trammel spawns as the ordering proxy. Run the Pillow-equipped project Python with `-B scripts/inspect-creature-mul-frames.py 56 42 28 39 21 47 30 14 26 4 13 48 74 15 86`. It verifies each direction against the ledger and writes private sheets and hashes under `work/creatures/mul-motion-review`. The body 56 source sheet shows a long-handled weapon held out during the gesture. The archive maps 56 to the plain skeleton mesh and 148 to the mage mesh. The recovered 13-vertex, 18-triangle Mage Staff references `weapons_512b.tga`. Both skeleton rigs have original grasp sockets. Three tested mage clips share 32 named tracks with the plain rig. A private comparison imported all 33 named tracks on the mage rig. Grip fit, source-motion comparison and corpse continuity are still open for body 56.

# Raptor and Slith ready stance evidence

The ten-frame UOP group 25 references show both creatures lifting their heads compared with group 1. Raptor drops its foreclaws and holds its tail close to horizontal. Slith raises its head slightly with a small tail motion. The one-second replacements keep the current licensed Raptor rig and Slithâ€™s recovered Komodo Dragon rig. The Raptorâ€™s first render lifted the tail too far. The corrected silhouette was rendered and retested before the final native run.

The ready stances passed twelve decoded routes through Classic slot 11 and newer type 14, preserving existing animation tracks and six static corpse hue cases. Across 726 sampled poses, the planted joints stayed fixed. The posed mesh added no penetration relative to idle on flat and four 14% test planes. The Raptorâ€™s raw pre-contact floor gap remains about 25 mm below the reference, as in its existing idle. The final isolated run passed 615 checks with Raptor, Slith, Stone Slith and Toxic Slith. Both clients witnessed each of the two actions for all four classes. Activation follows the existing 30 redirects and checks that both layers coexist.

To reproduce the bake and local test, run Godot with `--headless --path Viewer --script res://../scripts/bake-creature-ready-stance.gd` and then `test-creature-ready-stance.gd`. For the isolated native run, use `./scripts/test-horses.ps1 -Live -SkipContact -CreatureFamily ready_stances`, then verify the native receipt with `python -B scripts/accept-classic-gestures.py --batch ready_stances`.

# Classic UOP redirects prepared

Thirty body-specific redirects across Fire Gargoyle 130, Animated Weapon 692, Raptor 730, Slith 734 and Enslaved Gargoyle 753 are verified in the isolated preview. The recipe pins `AnimationSequence.uop`, `mobtypes.txt`, and the destination frame payloads. Each zero-frame redirect reuses its bodyâ€™s existing destination clip and keeps all scene assets and animation tracks intact. The preview passed 90 redirected decoded routes and 30 destination checks across the three mesh treatments. Godot logged a null-material cleanup diagnostic during the preview; the decoded action checks completed.

The isolated ModernUO run passed 1,705 checks and recorded 88 two-client redirect witnesses across all seven reviewed native classes. The run also verified movement, raw hues and persistent corpses or, for Animated Weapon, native corpse suppression. Activation is queued after the full regression run. Its local registry update is scoped to the verified rows and rolls back if an existing package gate fails. Reproduce with `python -B scripts/prepare-creature-uop-redirects.py`, then `./scripts/test-horses.ps1 -Live -SkipContact -CreatureFamily uop_redirects` and `python -B scripts/prepare-creature-uop-redirects.py --check-native`.

The two other redirects send Classic slot 11 to canonical group 25 on Raptor and Slith. In both sequence tables, group 25 is also the mode 1 default and standing group, and a type 14 action target. Each group has ten frames per direction. The decoded poses show a low ready stance and small head or tail motion. Across all five directions, neither body shares any identical decoded RGBA frame between group 25 and the ordinary group 1 idle. These two routes still need their own clip or a source motion with a matching pose.

No named compatible original 3D pillage clip was found in the checked Gargoyle motion set. An authored 1.3-second crouch and alternating reach now bakes on the archived slave rig with 13 reference poses sampled at 120 Hz. Five Forward+ frames rendered through the slot 11 candidate. Across 97 skeletal samples, the hips lowered 510 mm, maximum forward hand travel was 479 mm, and ankle drift stayed below 0.93 mm. The complete posed skin reached 2.05 mm below the flat reference before runtime contact correction. The baker now emits the original, rounded and modern candidates. All three passed 97 samples each and twelve decoded client routes in total, including Pillage, Fidget and both cast slots. The isolated ModernUO run passed 189 checks across two clients, including the fixture-requested Pillage action, raw hues, movement and persistent native corpse replication. The headless client also built static corpses for hues 0, 53 and 0x8035. The Gargoyle update is now active locally after 131 family action checks, 108 family route checks and the active three-treatment gesture and corpse tests. # Classic slot 11 choreography references

Decoded one complete five-frame direction for bodies 1, 3, 4, 42, 50, 53, 54 and 55 from `anim.mul`. They show different movements: Ogre raises a hand to its face, Zombie throws its arms up then stoops, Gargoyle advances with wing movement, Ratman shifts in a hunched posture, Skeleton reaches, and the three Troll forms sweep one arm overhead. Body 53 shows a bladed weapon in the later group 4â€“6 comparison, body 54 is unarmed, and body 55 carries a spiked weapon. The archived axe is the closest tested fit for body 53, and body 54 stays unarmed. The archived Frost Club has a smooth swollen head. Matching the Classic silhouette for body 55 requires adding its visible spikes. These are visual references for candidate motions. The frames do not establish which gameplay conditions send slot 11.

The first three separate replacements bake on the original Ogre, Zombie and Skeleton rigs, with 37, 34 and 35 joints. Body 1 uses FaceWipe, body 3 uses ArmsRaise, and body 50 uses Reach. Fifteen Forward+ frames and three decoded action routes passed. Across 183 sampled poses the native root stayed fixed and measured right-ankle drift stayed below 0.001 mm. The original, rounded and modern treatments then passed 549 sampled poses and nine decoded routes, retaining the existing track paths and keys. The isolated ModernUO run passed 535 checks on Ogre, Zombie, Skeleton and Spellbinder, including slot 11, movement, hues and corpses on both clients. These are authored interpretations. The body-scoped publication gate matches the tested packages and preserves the other Skeleton identities. The three routes are now active locally. The active tests passed nine decoded routes, 549 sampled poses and nine hue/corpse cases. The avatar successfully switched from body 50 to 56 and back, loading Reach only on body 50. The updated ledger has 5,150 routable and 1,688 unresolved protocol slots. Reproduce the native test with `./scripts/test-horses.ps1 -Live -SkipContact -CreatureFamily classic_gestures`. Run Godot with `--headless --path Viewer --script res://../scripts/bake-classic-gesture-trials.gd` then `--script res://../scripts/test-classic-gesture-trials.gd`.

The Troll source audit recovered four separately textured Third Dawn weapon meshes from the existing AOS extraction. Frost Club has 50 vertices and 96 triangles, standard Club 34 and 88, Axe 36 and 64, and Maul 34 and 60. All reference `weapons_512b.tga`. `trolls_troll_fidget2.grn` contains 35 bones and 35 animation tracks (SHA-256 `387142caf0dd1af9e1f731948f9438a4bff32ccbaac3d519cf96dc23562d9fb9`). All 35 Fidget2 tracks matched the original 38-joint standard and frost rigs. A Forward+ comparison at five phases per clip and body shows Fidget2 is a small idle shift. The archived Attack raises both arms while standing, whereas the Classic slot-11 frames show a kneeling one-arm gesture. Neither archival clip was assigned to slot 11. Reproduce the static export with `python -B scripts/prepare-troll-action-comparison.py PATH_TO_EXTRACTED_MODELS`. It writes the asset and input hashes under the ignored review folder and exports the four exact rigid meshes with their pinned atlas. Eight additional Forward+ images compared the weapon silhouettes.

The authored Troll KneelingSwing now bakes on the original standard and frost rigs. Bodies 53, 54 and 55 passed decoded slot 11 and raw partial-hue checks, with five Forward+ frames per body. In 363 sampled poses the authoritative root stayed fixed, hip drop reached 740 mm, the right wrist rose 458 mm above the head joint, and the lowest body vertex stayed within 0.001 mm of the flat reference. These are body-only trials. The recovered axe and the frost weapon still need grip, clearance and corpse checks. Reproduce with Godot `--headless --path Viewer --script res://../scripts/bake-troll-overhead-trial.gd` then `--script res://../scripts/test-troll-overhead-trial.gd`.

The armed Troll trials attach the archived axe to body 53 and the archived Frost Club to body 55 through the original `cp_grasp_rhand` socket. Four authored spikes restore the Frost Club silhouette as a separate material surface. Body 54 retains its unarmed route. Ten Forward+ frames cover the armed kneeling swing. Across 732 weapon poses, the smallest living flat-floor gaps were 603 mm for the axe and 493 mm for the Frost Club. During the original collapse, the axe reached 83 mm below the plane and the Frost Club reached 329 mm below it. Six static corpse cases matched every weapon corner to the final animated pose with the common corpse ground offset. The decoded Classic death groups 2 and 3 keep both weapons visible. The corrected collapse adds weapon settlement and adjusts three right-arm rotations when the grip needs to rise. The rest of the source collapse retains its existing tracks. At 481 sampled collapse poses per body, minimum flat-floor clearance was 10.9 mm for the axe and 8.2 mm for the club, and weapon-to-grip displacement stayed below 0.17 mm. Ten Forward+ death frames and all six static corpse cases passed again. The existing armed-creature terrain renderer passed 492 further collapse samples on four slopes and both sides of a 22 cm step per armed body. All twelve decoded corpse handoffs preserved the world-space weapon surfaces and picking bounds, including the Frost Club spikes. The Troll bakes now cover original, rounded and modern treatments. The isolated ModernUO run passed 403 checks on bodies 53, 54 and 55 with two clients. The test exercises the requested slot 11, movement, hues and native corpse replication. Run `./scripts/test-horses.ps1 -Live -SkipContact -CreatureFamily troll_gestures` after baking. The shared body-scoped publisher now verifies the exact Troll packages and all three model treatments. Activation is queued after the full registry verifier. After the source comparison export and body bake, run Godot `--headless --path Viewer --script res://../scripts/bake-troll-weapons-trial.gd` and then `--script res://../scripts/test-troll-weapon-trial.gd`.

The action ledger now lists source-backed candidates separately from all unresolved protocol slots. At this checkpoint, 67 distinct unresolved classic body/slot pairs have a compatible MUL layout or a numerically matching UOP frame record. Canonical UOP groups still need a wire-category review. The next direct UOP comparisons are Blistering Gargoyle 130, Troglodyte 267 and Parrot 831.

# Corpser emergence active

Classic body 8 slot 12 shows a plant emerging through the ground. The archived Corpser has seven named model and motion members plus its atlas, with no dedicated emergence member found. The replacement adds a 1.1-second `Emerge` clip by sampling its original Idle pose and animating the original rig's vertical entry. The visual review used a flat soil surface. The current candidate omits the original sprite's debris.

The actual candidate client routed slot 12 and passed 15 terrain poses across flat ground and two slopes. Three interruptions reached the same posed bounds as an unmodified Attack sample. The static corpse still built with a partial hue. Five Vulkan frames showed the growth from fully occluded to standing. The candidate is prepared with `python -B scripts/prepare-corpser-emergence.py`. The normal registry passed the same checks. The isolated flail-creature suite passed 495 checks, and both clients selected Emerge for Corpser group 12. The fixture sent the group explicitly.

It found 91 structurally readable canonical groups across seven bodies in the installed archives. Body.def precedence and original visual choreography remain separate.

# All of Trammel baked in the compact streaming format â€” 31 October 2026

Owner request: rebake the whole Trammel map (dungeons included) in the compact format, then clean up the old caches.

**Bake format** (rule and details: [COMPACT_STREAMING.md](COMPACT_STREAMING.md)):
- placed models are instanced from a shared model library, not merged;
- detailed solids collide as boxes, and terrain as a heightmap;
- facets bake out of core in windows (`scripts/bake-facet.ps1`) and are published by `FinalizeFacet.gd`.

**Result:**
- all 7,168 districts, 14 GB: districts 7.08 GB, library 7.3 GB, far 0.05 GB;
- the old format would have needed over 200 GB;
- the library deduplication is the next fix, with about 7 GB to gain.

**Checks:**
- `scripts/test-streaming.ps1 -Route trinsic` passes on Trammel: 54 stops, a memory plateau of about 840 MB, p95 frame 30 ms;
- renders of Moonglow and the Covetous area;
- new `CompactStreamingTests` (6/0) in verify;
- DevEditTests 78/0.

**Fixes found along the way:**
- **Developer terrain editing:** it now maps local terrain vertices to tiles through the district node's offset. Edits would otherwise have landed on the wrong tiles in the new cache.
- **Tool paths:** developer-editing tools and audits read the active cache through `LiveDistricts.prepared_folder()`.

The full verify passes.

**Not yet:** a live walk through the new map in the game client.

# Paperdoll replaces the early character sheet; all of Trammel populated on the server â€” 31 October 2026

Owner decisions: remove the early character sheet, and extend the world to all of Trammel, client and server.

**Character sheet retired:**
- The vitals card (top left), Character (C), the Status action and K no longer open the early character sheet, whose 3D portrait showed the mount. The card, C and Status open your paperdoll; K opens the Skills window.
- The paperdoll gained the sheet's one unique feature: Str/Dex/Int with the server's gain lock per stat (raise, lower, locked; a click cycles it and sends the lock), plus the stat total over the cap.
- The sheet's code stays only for two live capture scripts.
- Tests: PolishTests 46/0 (stats with their locks, a click cycles and sends, no locks on another character).

**Server, all of Trammel (`Server/PreviewTools/PreviewTrammel.cs`, `[PreviewTrammel`, `scripts/populate-trammel.ps1`):**
- Uses ModernUO's own content, Trammel only: decorations (Britannia and Trammel lists, with the upstream duplicate check), doors over the whole facet (the bounded door pass, never replacing a door), signs (signs.cfg), teleporters (teleporters.json), the public moongates on ModernUO's Trammel list, and the standard spawners.
- Existing items are kept, so a repeat adds nothing. `[PreviewSave` saves on demand.
- Run live: decorations, doors, signs, teleporters and moongates over the whole facet in about a minute. The item index grew 5.4 times and the mobile index 8.4 times. The pre-population save is in `Server/runtime/Backups/BeforeTrammel-20261031-133612`.
- **Known issue:** the scripted client is dropped while the server is busy, so the result line is lost. `TrammelLive.gd` now waits patiently for it, but the reply still needs to be logged on the server.

**Client, all of Trammel:**
- The facet is converted: `outputs/trammel`, 7,168 districts of which 4,164 are empty, 2.95 million statics, 138 MB.
- LiveDistricts streams `outputs/trammel` by default once it has been baked.
- **Baking is on hold:** the prepared streaming format bakes each district as one merged, uncompressed mesh. A forest district near Trinsic is 204 MB, and Britain to Trinsic (494 districts) is 16 GB, so all of Trammel would need roughly 60 to 120 GB. The cache has to be made compact first: shared models placed by reference (instances) and compressed resources.

**Server start, two environment problems:**
- `Start-Server.cmd` launched from PowerShell 7 hides `Get-FileHash` from Windows PowerShell 5.1; clear PSModulePath first.
- Codex's in-progress `scripts/install-server-fixes.ps1` uses `[IO.File]::Move(a,b,$true)` and then `[IO.File]::Replace(a,b,$null)`, both unavailable in Windows PowerShell 5.1. The verified, receipted UOContent.dll was installed by hand instead.

# Houses: hipped roofs, narrow roof strips, per-cell gables; server restarted â€” 31 October 2026

- **Server restarted:** the ActionTimers extension is installed and loaded (quickbar dials now follow ModernUO's real waits).

# Houses, batch 3: every classic house is a whole model â€” 31 October 2026

`HouseModel` now covers all 19 classic house multis ModernUO builds (Houses.cs):
- the five small houses;
- the small shops 0xA0/0xA2;
- the guild house 0x74 and the two-storey houses 0x76/0x78;
- the large patio house 0x8C and the sandstone patio house 0x9C;
- the marble house 0x96, the small tower 0x98, the log cabin 0x9A and the villa 0x9E;
- the tower 0x7A, the keep 0x7C and the castle 0x7E.

**Storey model, all from the tiles:**
- **Storeys:** wall tiles are grouped by the height they stand on. Each wall cell rises to its own tiles' top, so a loft railing stays half height. Doors, windows, timber framing and corners are worked out per storey. Upper floors are thick slabs, and a balcony shows its soffit.
- **Short wall tiles:**
  - under a pitched roof, those at the eaves fill the gables;
  - elsewhere they are walls of their own height: a shop's arcade piers, courtyard flower beds, balustrades;
  - a storey made only of them is a parapet round a flat roof (towers, keep, castle, terraces) with a flagged deck.
  - Foundation courses (short pieces ending at ground-floor level) stay in the plinth, so doorways stay open.
- **Stairs:**
  - stairs and landings inside climb tile by tile, each a block to its own top with a tread;
  - an outside flight up to a terrace stands on the ground;
  - front steps stay as before.
- **Doorways** only between full-height walls, never in a flower bed.

**Materials:**
- log walls are stacked round logs (one merged mesh per wall piece, CC0 `rough_wood`);
- marble is large cream marble blocks (CC0 `marble_01`), without quoins;
- fortifications (stone houses of 120 or more wall cells) are coursed castle masonry (CC0 `castle_brick_01`); cottages keep fieldstone;
- the chimney sits on the main roof block.
- Also pinned but unused: `castle_brick_02_white`, tried for marble and rejected.

**Tests:** WorldItemTests 70/70. All 19 build with a doorway and a roof or parapet storey; the two-storey house has both storeys and a staircase; the log cabin has log walls; the castle has several storeys. `BakeHouses.gd` bakes all 19, and the full verify passes.

**Not yet:**

- custom (designed) houses still draw tile by tile;
- castle doorways carry the cottage door canopy, a candidate for a stone hood later.

# Houses, batch 2: all small houses, the guild house and the patio house â€” 31 October 2026

The owner approved the whole-house approach on the small house; it now covers seven of ModernUO's classic houses.
- **Supported:**
  - the five small houses: stone-and-plaster 0x64, fieldstone 0x66, brick 0x68, wood 0x6A, wood-and-plaster 0x6C;
  - the large brick guild house 0x74;
  - the large patio house 0x8C.
- **Materials from the tiles' own names:**
  - roof covering: clay tiles, or wooden shingles (new CC0 `roof_09`);
  - corners: sandstone quoins, or oak corner posts where the layout has wooden posts or wood walls;
  - walls: fieldstone (new CC0 `stone_wall`), brick, plaster with an oak frame, or wood siding (new CC0 `weathered_plank_siding`);

- **Builder generalised for larger layouts:**
  - roofs are split into rectangles over the roof cells, each a gable at the height its tiles reach, so the L-shaped patio house gets two gables meeting in a valley;

  - floors outside the roof (the patio) are stone flags;
  - corner posts and quoins sit on real L corners of the walls, never in an open patio.
- **Unmodelled parts keep their tile models:** fences, counters, benches, flowers and posts in a house layout (`HouseModel.consumes`). The patio house keeps its 22 such parts.
- **Baking:** `BakeHouses.gd` bakes all seven to the ignored `local-multis/houses`.

# Closed bags, status-bar menus, Equip, server-opened backpack â€” 31 October 2026

Owner reports from live play:
- **Closed reagent bag:**
  - The client only knows a bag's contents once the server has sent them (0x3C). The session now records those containers (`seen_containers`).
  - While you carry a bag never seen inside, a zero count means "not seen", not "missing". Spells are not refused for reagents (the server checks every bag), and the spellbook shows "?" or "in a closed bag?" instead of "missing".
  - Item, potion and bandage slots that need the exact item open the unseen bags first (up to four, 0.6 s apart for ModernUO's action delay), then use the item.
- **Status bars act like the creature in the world:** a single click on the target frame, a pulled-out bar or a party card selects the creature and opens its server menu (pet orders, vendor Buy/Sell). Hovering shows its server tooltip (name and property lines, requested once) above the bar's hint.
- **Backpack Equip:** the item card's actions add Equip for anything with a paperdoll layer (clothing, armour, jewellery, weapons, shields, tools).
- **Open Backpack from the self menu:** the server opening your pack now shows the modern backpack window. It was ignored before; only B opened it. B is the menu bar's toggle alone now, so closing the pack no longer reopens it.

**Tests:**
- QuickbarTests 58/0: unopened bag, then known-missing reagents.
- PolishTests 43/0: a bar's single click asks for the server menu.

# Pet orders, tool targeting, real action timers, worn tools â€” 31 October 2026

Owner requests from live play:
- **Pet menu:** a single click on any creature, horses included, now asks the server for its context menu. ModernUO lists a pet's orders for its owner (Kill, Follow, Guard, Stop, Stay, Drop, then Rename, Add/Remove Friend, Transfer, Release) and Follow/Stop/Stay for a pet friend. The menu shows them under "Orders" as plain verbs, then the rest under "Pet". A blank cliloc (Rename, 3006098) falls back to its name.
- **Target cursor:**
  - Every server target cursor shows the reticle (tools, skills, bandages), not only spells.
  - A click over no server object answers with the map static under the mouse: a location target (0x6C type 1) with the static's cell, z and graphic, as ModernUO's StaticTarget check expects, so a hatchet takes a tree. Surfaces are sent at their top, the High Seas rule.
  - The mouse ray is walked through each cell's tiledata height. Because our 3D crowns reach past the trunk's cell, a tall static one cell beside the ray counts. With no static, the ground (LandTarget, graphic 0) is sent.
- **Real action timers:**
  - New server extension `Server/PreviewTools/ActionTimers.cs`. It is read-only and on by default (`ultimaClone.actionTimers`). Every 100 ms it reads ModernUO's own NextSkillTime, the casting spell's start plus cast delay and recovery (else NextSpellTime), the BandageContext's remaining time and the heal potion lock.
  - It sends `UCWAIT1|kind|ms` when one changes; the client hides the line. The quickbar dials then show the real wait, whatever started the action (quickbar, spellbook, skills window, a bandage double-click): Peacemaking's 30 s while targeting, then 10 s or 5 s, early resets, faster casting.
  - Without the extension the estimates remain.
- **Worn items:**
  - Items worn at login (0x78) are now known items, so they can be used.
  - Quickbar use-item slots look in your hands and on your body before the backpack, so a held hatchet works.
  - Paperdoll items drag onto the quickbar.

**Tests:**
- PolishTests 42/0: pet menu grouping and reply.
- WorldItemTests 45/45: tree static and ground location targets, byte for byte.
- QuickbarTests 56/0: server waits replace estimates, held lock, UCWAIT1 parsing, worn hatchet found.
- The extension builds.

**Not yet:**
- The extension is not loaded on the running server: its DLL is locked, so it needs a saved, graceful restart and then `scripts/build-preview-tools.ps1`.
- Not yet checked live with a hatchet on a tree.

- **Click to raise:** a left or right press on a window or gump brings it to the front.
- **Spellbook:** using a spellbook opens the casting window on that book's school. No extra HUD button was added (P already opens it).
- **Blood** (ModernUO's Blood item, 0x1645 and 0x122A-0x122F) is a projected decal now, with generated albedo/normal/roughness maps (six variants: pooled dark centre, wet rim, droplets, streaks). It spreads in over a quarter second with a short spray of drops, and it lies on terrain and floors, so it no longer flickers.
- **Creature bars:**
  - The target frame is smaller. Click it to answer a waiting target cursor (or select), double-click it in war mode to attack, and drag it out to leave a bar for that creature.
  - Dragging a creature (or yourself) in the world also leaves its bar (`MobileBar.gd`): small, movable, notoriety-coloured, red-edged while you fight it and gold-edged when selected. It dims out of sight and closes on right-click, with the same click and double-click actions. Party cards and your own vitals card answer target cursors too.
- **Trees:** the see-through circle is gone. A tree whose crown stands on the line from the camera to the player (up to just past them), or that the camera is within about 3 m of, fades its whole crown out with an ordered dither by its own origin (per instance in a MultiMesh). The trunk and branches stay, and shadows keep the full tree.
- **No movement-blocked toasts:** a refused step just snaps back quietly.

**Tests:**
- PolishTests 40/0: right-click close, raise on click, target bar target/attack/drag-out, pulled bar attack and close.
- GumpTests 19/19: a closable gump's right-click sends button 0.
- WorldItemTests 43/43: blood decal.
- A scratch render check of the foliage shader: an occluding crown is gone and its bark is kept; a far tree or one behind the player is full.
- The full verify passes.

**Not yet:** checked live in a fight. No drag of a creature bar from party cards.

# Whole-creature programme / complete source ostard family - 30 October 2026

Owner approved the desert result and requested all creature families, equines first. [CREATURE_MODERNISATION.md](CREATURE_MODERNISATION.md) now tracks the whole programme and source-family queue. Forest, frenzied and ethereal source forms each have their own generated high-detail atlas, 2,960-triangle refined mesh and seven imported source clips. Ethereal uses a separate translucent/emissive shader; raw 0x4001 is not treated as dye 1. All four forms: 27,741 checks / 1,008 sampled skinned poses; new forms each pass real Forward+ and Compatibility material-response tests. Added 93 actual stills / 288 sequence frames; combined review at outputs/ostard-trial/family.html. Full verify exits 0 with existing unrelated bridge/Unicode/teardown diagnostics; final spectral assertions rerun on all forms.

The read-only whole Animals/Monsters source audit finds 147 LOD2 meshes / 51 filename-based groups, eight still unparsed and six parsed candidates with absent texture references. These are not server body coverage counts. Ki-rin diagnostic repair identifies six invalid normals attached only to unused entries or two repeated-vertex zero-area faces; removing the two faces leaves 137 vertices / 270 triangles / 30 bones with positions, UV coordinates, bones and weights preserved. Referenced kirin.tga is absent from this source archive; no textured export or working creature is claimed. All other families remain explicitly queued; llama/other animals follow distinctive equines. Rider fit, live ostard routes, terrain support and all-creature action/corpse integration remain pending. Art, provenance, prompts and captures remain local; only code/recipes/notes are committed. Concurrent UI/item/server dev-edit work is preserved and excluded.

# Desert ostard original-model trial - 30 October 2026

Implemented the first distinctive-creature upgrade comparison from the pinned Third Dawn archive: original geometry/atlas, geometry-only refinement and a new higher-detail atlas/PBR treatment. Source 98 vertices / 185 triangles -> refined 1,490 vertices / 2,960 triangles; same 24-bone rig and seven source clips. Display height 2.2 m; masked smoothing cap 55 mm; original UV seams and normalised skin weights retained. Built-in imagegen edited the 128-square atlas into a 1,254-square local texture; exact input/output/recipe hashes and final prompt are recorded locally. [Trial, sources and reproduction](OSTARD_TRIAL.md).

Reviews/Review-Ostard.cmd opens the interactive comparison; outputs/ostard-trial/index.html contains 31 real captures and walk/run GIFs assembled from 96 real frames at source clip timing. Full scripts/verify.ps1 exits 0, retaining the known two unrelated bridge missing-id diagnostics, malformed-Unicode/teardown and nullable warnings. No live login tests were needed.

Source rotations/durations are retained, but horizontal root motion is removed and a flat-floor lowest-vertex correction is baked, including attack/jump translation. Owner art review precedes male/female rider/outfit fitting, native identity routing and independent other-ostard tests. Ki-rin non-finite parser blocker remains. Human/horse presentation and coverage stay unchanged (wearables 732/1,126; equines 15/26 bodies plus two item-only ethereals). Source/derived art and captures remain ignored; code/recipes/notes only go to Git. Concurrent house/terrain/item work is preserved.

# Small house rebuilt as a whole house from its own layout â€” 30 October 2026

Owner direction: try the small house first, built with our own materials by copying the existing multi layout, and baked; then do the others progressively.

**`HouseModel.gd`** builds a house multi as one coherent building from its component list (the same layout the server uses) and the photoscanned surfaces in `Art/Surfaces`:
- **Plinth, floors and walls:**
  - a stone plinth to floor level with a dressed cap course, and plank floors;
  - walls 0.32 m thick on the wall cells' centre lines, so they stand exactly where the server's walls are. Arms run to wall neighbours, and window and door openings are left real.
- **Windows:** oak frames, a cross mullion, leaded glass, stone sills and painted shutters (the colour is seeded per house).
- **Doorway:** a gap between two wall cells in a line becomes the doorway, with an oak frame, a threshold stone and a pent canopy on brackets. The opening is 2.52 m, the server door's DoorwayPlan opening, so the server's own door leaf hangs exactly in it; its header pieces sit inside the thicker wall.
- **Plaster houses:** an oak frame (sole plate, mid rail, wall plate, corner posts, opening trimmers, studs and braces) over limewash, with sandstone quoins.
- **Roof:**
  - the ridge follows the topmost roof tiles (anchor-shifted) and the eaves sit at the lowest;
  - clay tiles with overhang, fascia, bargeboards and a ridge cap;
  - timber-framed gable ends.
- **Chimney and steps:** a stone chimney with pots and smoke, and stone front steps with dressed treads and cheek walls.

**Baking and use:**
- `BakeHouses.gd` saves each supported multi to the ignored `local-multis/houses/<id>.scn`. It is derived from the install layout, so it is never committed.
- `MultiPresenter` uses the baked scene, or builds the house on the fly, instead of the tiles. The tiles still feed movement, and server doors stay server items.
- `HouseDressing` keeps the window boxes, lanterns, garden and ivy around it, without a second chimney.

**Scope:** `SUPPORTED = [0x64]` (small stone-and-plaster house) for owner review before the other house types.

**Tests:** `WorldItemTests` checks that the small house arrives through the real multi path as one house with a doorway and roof, that it keeps its dressing with exactly one chimney, and that its wall cells still feed movement (42/42). The full verify passes.

**Not yet:** checked in a live game on a placed house.

# Ships and houses â€” 30 October 2026

Owner request: full houses and ships, "surprise me".

**Ships: scanned models.** All six classic boats (ModernUO ships no High Seas multis) now draw CC-BY Sketchfab ships, pinned through the Sketchfab API:

| Boat | Model |
|---|---|
| Small | caravel |
| Medium | the Nao Victoria |
| Large | weathered three-masted galleon |
| Dragon boats | dragon-prow Norse longship with shields along the rail |

- **Fit:** each model is fitted to the boat's footprint length and turned to its facing. Its deck is lifted to the server deck (DECK_Z above the boat), using deck heights measured by ray casts onto each hull, so passengers stand on the planking.
- **`ShipMotion`:**
  - the hull rides a gentle swell (heave, roll and pitch), which is small at deck level and smaller on bigger ships;
  - a foam wake trails from the stern, and spray curls off the bow while under way;
  - a warm stern lantern is lit.

  The root, server position and movement cells are untouched.
- **Closed gangplanks** are hidden, since the hull's rail stands in for them, but stay clickable. They had drawn a rowboat scan.
- **Fallback:** the procedural ships remain for clients without the pinned art.
- **Tooling:** `blender-objaverse-reduce.py` clears shape keys before decimating, because animated sails blocked reduction.
- **Rejected after review:** a blocky carrack scan and a plain Snekkar.

**Houses: lived-in dressing.** Classic and custom houses keep the shell built tile by tile from the server layout. Floors, interior doors, stairs and multi-storey rooms are what players walk, open and decorate, so a store-bought shell cannot match them. The original art also mixes materials on purpose (the stone-and-plaster small house). `HouseDressing` adds, outside the walls and on the roof, seeded per house:
- a stone chimney with drifting smoke on the ridge end;
- flower window boxes under the windows;
- hanging lanterns on iron posts either side of the front steps;
- shrubs and flowers along the sides and back;
- ivy up two masonry corners.

**Open:**
- full-model exteriors for the simple small houses, swapped for the shell when the player is inside, need a decision on door handling;
- the garden flowers read small;
- ships and dressing were checked in rendered scenes, not yet on a live server boat or house.

`WorldItemTests` covers the caravel, longship, swaying hull and stern lantern (39/39).

# Horse corpses use the horse model â€” 30 October 2026

Owner request: use the new horse model as the base for horse corpses.

- **How it works:** a corpse item (0x2006) carries its creature's body in the amount field. When that body is one of the four ordinary horse coats, `WorldItems._equine_corpse` draws the shared horse model (local `warhorse`) with that coat and hue. It holds the last frame of the authored death clip, fixed in place: no reins, gait or ground-contact sway, a deterministic yaw per serial and a pick and contact body.
- **Unchanged:** other creatures' corpses keep their generic look until their models exist.
- **Test:** `WorldItemTests` checks that a horse corpse holds the final death pose and that a human corpse is unaffected (37/37).
- **Known gap:** the warhorse asset bakes its saddle and tack into the body mesh, so a horse corpse still wears its tack.
- **Expanded equines:** nightmares, steeds and unicorns follow once the Codex equine registry lands (currently it uses the committed `HorseVisual.BODIES`).
- **Process note:** while reverting a temporary edit, the Codex session's uncommitted `HorseVisual.gd` changes were discarded by `git checkout`. They were rebuilt line for line from earlier reads; every function sits at its original line and `EquineTests` passes 4,477/4,477. The file is left uncommitted for that session.
- **Codex WIP fails a committed test:** `HorsePolishTests` reports 32 failures against the work-in-progress registry names ("Horse / golden tan" against "golden tan"). This is outside this change.

# Spell scrolls share one look â€” 30 October 2026

Owner direction: spell scrolls reuse the same models with small variations of hue and size.

`PropReplacements.spell_scroll(id)` covers all 113 scroll graphics by range: Magery 0x1F2Dâ€“0x1F6C, Necromancy 0x2260â€“0x2270, Spellweaving 0x2D51â€“0x2D60 and Mysticism 0x2D9Eâ€“0x2DAD.
- **Look:** a tied bundle of two or three rolls made from the three scroll scans already pinned (old scroll, ancient scroll, rolled parchment), laid flat side by side.

- `tint` multiplies a scan's own colours while keeping its texture.
- `spread` sets the cluster distance.
- `aligned` lays cluster copies parallel along each model's long axis.

The 113 graphics are approved. Most scrolls appear only in packs and on vendors, so the placed-graphics count moves by 6. The world stands at 559 open; Britain and Trinsic stay at 100%.

# Trinsic bridges: timber footbridges and solid stone piers â€” 29 October 2026

Owner report: a Trinsic bridge "looks really bad", and asked to look for a free model. Captures showed two defects. Trinsic's six canal footbridges were flat boxes on stilts with loose rail pieces. The long sandstone east bridge (2067â€“2104, 2790â€“2800) had wooden balustrades on its pier tops and hollow, slotted piers.

**Model search.** Sketchfab and the Objaverse mirror were searched through the existing pinning pipeline (CC0 / CC-BY 4.0 only), with eight bridge queries and 33 licensed candidates; Poly Haven was also checked. None fits a UO bridge footprint. The candidates are single fixed-proportion spans (stylised, low-poly or multi-million-triangle scans), while UO bridges are tile-built at exact walking heights and every crossing has its own length, width and ramps. Poly Haven's CC0 `modular_wooden_pier` and `modular_fort_01` kits are the best free matches for later dock and tower work.

**Shared rules instead of a model:**
- `TimberBridge.gd` recognises a deck of boards over water with a ramp as one footbridge. Docks without ramps keep their pieces. The bridge is drawn once, by its first deck board, fitted to its own footprint:
  - weathered boards (pinned CC0 Poly Haven `rough_wood`) laid along the walking surface, ramps included and gently rounded;
  - stringers and side fascias;
  - trestle bents with cap beams and cross bracing, down to the bank or riverbed;
  - sills, and post-and-rail handrails on both sides.
  Its members are accounted as components, and source XYZ is unchanged.
- **Solid piers.** A closed 2Ã—2 ring of masonry wall pieces under a paved top is drawn as solid blocks. This applies to bridge piers and to paved thick-wall walks.
- **Stairwell rails.** They no longer appear on a pier top, and a stone block paved on top counts as a foundation, not a stairwell step.
- **Stacked landings.** A stack of stone blocks draws one paved top.

**Evidence:**
- Before and after captures of every Trinsic footbridge, the east bridge, the docks, Britain castle and the Trinsic west wall. These are local captures from preview bakes, not committed.
- A whole-region comparison (britain-trinsic) against the previous code: stairwell rails go from 245 to 139. Every one removed is in Trinsic bridge, footbridge or pier footprints; none is in Britain. 662 pier blocks; 6 footbridges with 580 records.
- `BridgeTests.gd`: 15 checks, run in verify.

**Remaining:** Trinsic's sandstone still reads dark brown, because of the regional art tint on `medieval_blocks_03`. The east bridge stays a solid causeway, as the source stacks it. The docks keep their individual planks and posts.

# Commit, persistent rules and talking to the world â€” 29 October 2026

Roadmap items 1â€“3 done plus the owner's follow-up.

- **Talking to the world.** An Ask box (typed, or Windows voice typing) feeds an offline interpreter that maps requests to the shared command grammar and never guesses; everything else goes to Claude Code through a server-side ask/answer/propose queue and `scripts/dev-edit-bridge.ps1`. A real round trip answered a developer's request with a scoped proposal.
- **Group edits.** Bounded rule scopes and shift-click multi-select apply as atomic batches with one undo.
- **Commit.** Session statics, placements and terrain fold into local committed overrides that the converter applies on every bake. Collision is kept consistent with ModernUO; only touched districts rebake, and running clients reload them live: 75 s bake, 2 districts, worst frame 146 ms. Persistent "always/never" data rules are supported; behaviour rules such as roof/wall attachment are routed to Claude as code changes. Each Commit is now also published to Git as one change file on a `world-edits/<id>` branch with a pull request, built by plumbing (no checkout), and merged edits apply on collaborators' next launch. Server-only state stays on the server. Evidence: commit converter tests 20/20, including published merging, skipping a published local copy, restore-over-hide and terrain built on published edits. A dry-run publish produced exactly one added file on `origin/main` with the working tree untouched, and the GitHub API credential has push rights. A real push and pull request is not yet exercised: it will happen on the first in-game Commit.
- **Talk (local speech).** The F9 panel records the microphone in game and recognizes it with local Whisper (whisper.cpp, 99 languages, offline). It has a language picker, optional translation into English commands, and a microphone picker. An 8 s spoken command is recognized in about 1.6 s on the CPU. AI edits follow the manual flow: the assistant role can only propose, and plans are drawn as drafts before Accept (live 41/41). Unknown requests go to Claude automatically, and long answers arrive in parts. See DEV_EDIT.md.
- **Code fix previews.** A converter fix is judged in the world before it becomes code. `scripts/code-preview.ps1` copies `Viewer` (code copied, art hard-linked, in 30 s) and bakes only the affected districts into `outputs/<region>/previews/<id>/`. A `codepreview <id>` proposal opens a card in the F9 panel: those districts swap in place, Before/After compares, beacons mark examples, and Accept tells Claude to make it permanent. Live check 6/6: 7 districts swapped and back, worst frame 98 ms. The first case, walls poking through sloped roofs (owner report), affects 981 walls in 36 districts; it is baked as a preview awaiting the owner's decision.
- **Previews and revert.** Assets follow the cursor and replacements preview in place; `revert-commits.ps1` rolls back.

Tests: server 65, client 72 (headless and GPU), converter 15, live 40; verify.ps1 and test-login.ps1 pass. See DEV_EDIT.md.

# Live collaborative developer editing â€” 28 October 2026

Owner goal: build and fix the world from inside it, together, and eventually just by talking (Claude Code now, voice later). Delivered a working vertical slice in live Britain. F9 opens a developer panel that joins a server-authoritative edit session in the isolated ModernUO extension (`[DevEdit` over the native connection; explicit account allowlist plus loopback; separate journal, never world saves). Supported: select and inspect statics (identity, coordinates, prepared source record), place approved decorative assets, move/rotate/replace/hide existing decorative statics, and bounded terrain height/surface brushes. The converted bake stays immutable. It now records per-static render ownership, so edits collapse or restore only that static's geometry, and terrain deforms only affected district meshes on a worker thread, crack-free across borders. `scripts/dev-edit.ps1` is a Claude Code bridge using the same command model.

Two graphical clients saw each other's edits in 134â€“256 ms; the cross-border brush rebuilt 2 of 9 districts; worst edit frame 72 ms against a 19â€“45 ms two-client baseline. verify.ps1 and test-login.ps1 pass. Refit robe/skirt/kilt from anatomical pelvis/leg samples, excluding A-pose fingers; compress deep pleats and rebake the existing controls while retaining full legs, body/UV/bind and sleeves. Matched Idle thigh widths: skirts 28.3â€“35.8% narrower, kilts 23.4â€“36.9%; female robe 10.8% narrower, male robe within 0.4% of its prior width. Added CC0 civilian shirt, soft cap and cloth hat on both bodies (16 equipment categories); rejected the bodice/doublet candidate after layered review. Native hue/partial-hue updates preserve independent hair/beard colours and walk phase. Two fidgets bring each body to 45 clips and 23/35 classic groups connected.

Reviews/Review-Human-Catalogue.cmd generates a searchable local worklist: 1,126 indexed wearable graphics (84 category-mapped), all 35 classic human groups, 17 selected human/equipment UOP sequence records with 39 body / 293 equipment mode-action rows and 39,410 item/action review rows. Raw MUL availability, category art, per-item fidelity and pending QA are separate. Non-human candidates are flagged; mounted human groups are deferred. Sources, archive/member hashes, reproduction, evidence and gaps: HUMAN_WEARABLE_CATALOGUE.md.

Validation: 102 exported models, 90 motion clips with body error below 0.000001 m, 816 modern cloth poses, 192 native skirt/kilt + 96 native robe poses; 8 parser, 20 new wardrobe/dye and 60 motion checks. Full project verification passes. Existing dedicated server characters pass re-entry, robe removal/re-equip (including the new female shirt/kilt), reconnect, observer appearance/action updates, war/peace/bow and native login/relay. Logs: work/human-wardrobe-batch/verify-accepted.log, live-accepted.log, final-motion7.log and asset-checks6.log. Artistic approval, exact item shapes, some layered waist overlap/deep pleats, two-handed/ranged/turn/salute, racial class audit and broader skin hue fidelity remain open. All artwork/captures/native data and test accounts stay ignored; concurrent world work is preserved.

WeaponGrip now fits finger curls/thumb opposition on the fifteen right finger bones; it leaves the wrist/free hand animated and releases on unequip. Full verification passes; Forward+/Compatibility hand views inspected. See HUMAN_ARM_MOTION.md and work/human-grip/verify.log. Exact per-handle contact, left/two-handed equipment and robe drape remain open. Concurrent world work stays separate.

Both bodies remain 43 clips on the same mesh/61-joint bind, with garment-safe footwork and authoritative server state unchanged. New review snapshots compare the immediately preceding prepared motion with this revision. Owner quality acceptance, inflated-looking robe poses and exact hammer/sceptre grip fitting remain open; concurrent world/prop edits are separate.

# Britain defect sweep: shops, windows, arches, doors, rugs â€” 27 October 2026

- Counters follow their real footprint cell by cell (an L counter had become one bounding-box table) with a panelled photoscanned-oak carcass; the stand-in Quaternius cabinet is gone. Display-case blocks are one glazed cabinet per cell (timber carcass, felt bed, rim, thin non-depth-writing glass) instead of one case stretched over 5x3 m; the flicker came from that stretched depth-pre-pass glass.
- Server decoration (potted plants, goods) rests on the presented surface by a downward ray; reviewed floor/surface items get the same final correction. Potted plants/trees are pinned CC0 Poly Haven scans (potted_plant_02/04); chests and metal boxes are iron-bound photoscanned chests.
- Generic "window"/"archway" graphics take the family of the wall run they sit in (log/plank houses lost their white plaster window panels; the Wayfarer's Inn gallery is a timber-framed opening, castle gates stay stone). Brick "arched" windows are glazed leaded casements under a brick segmental arch. Wall-top "stone pillar" sprites are slim pinnacles (Chamber of Virtue), not 4 m piers. Signpost stubs are no longer drawn (the server sign builds its bracket).
- Doors have jambs and a lintel; over-door infill continues posts and bressumer in timber walls. Plaster albedo lowered (no clipping to flat white in sun). Terrain may meet a wall 0.6 m above its base, so streets no longer form trenches along facades.
- Rugs: woven damask field (floral_jacquard), pile and relief from dirty_carpet, guard stripes and border on each rug's true outer edges (CC0 Poly Haven, pinned).
- Replacement pipeline started: PropReplacements.gd + prop-replacements.json map UO item names to pinned CC0 Poly Haven scans at real heights (statics and server items); wave 1 covers barrels, plates, bowls, buckets, baskets, apples, pots, stools, thrones, grandfather clocks; chests of drawers use GothicCommode_01. Rejected after side-by-side review: bare pale bookshelf, human statue for UO "statue" (eagle and others), spinning wheel (rendered empty). Chairs, bookcases and garbage (the largest groups) still need suitable sources.
- Walls standing a few Z above their plinth, floor or ground get a stone foundation course down to it (the daylight slot under raised houses such as 1491,1601-1625). Beds use the carved Poly Haven GothicBed_01 instead of the pink Quaternius/Kenney beds; server chests of drawers use GothicCommode_01; in modern mode candelabra and candles use the owner packs. Rug pattern contrast reduced.
- Museum/Sketchfab scans via the public Objaverse mirror: scripts/add-objaverse.py checks each licence on the Sketchfab API (CC0 or CC-BY 4.0 only), pins source and output SHA-256, reduces in Blender (scripts/blender-objaverse-reduce.py) and writes Viewer/Art/Objaverse/NOTICE.md attribution. Wave 2: chairs (carved folk chair, rush-seat chair; facings measured from geometry), book-filled bookcases, carved wardrobe, brass candelabra, Mary Rose tankard, chalice, anvil, iron chandelier, Galway fireplace (last two pinned, not yet placed). Reviewed scans outrank owner-pack stand-ins in modern mode. Rugs on several storeys of the same cell keep one border. Rejected after preview: park bench, tree-slice table, food-laden table scene, untextured stoneware jug, ore sack, plain candle holder.
- Owner report round: open armoires (ModernUO swaps 0xA4D/A4F/A51/A53 to their open graphics) keep their model instead of vanishing (regression check added); every free-standing server item rests its lowest mesh point on the surface below (pack candelabra sank), bake-time settling also raises sunken objects; rocks/logs no longer sway; arcades draw slim columns with arches springing between them, and arch openings span empty cells up to their pier (main gate, Chamber of Virtue, Sorcerer's Delight); bridge parapet runs share one deck-side offset; trunkless tree crowns become realistic trees of their species; the status panel shows the player tile.
- Training dummies (Medieval Combat Dummy, CC-BY) and hay (photoscanned hay pile, CC-BY) replace invisible stand-ins; scan replacements outrank pack slots. Upper floors get a timber balustrade on edges that drop into a stairwell (not where stairs arrive). Distance: depth fog 70-700 m with aerial perspective plus a gentle far depth-of-field blur. Shop signs can mount on arcade columns and piers.
- Door facings derived from door sets when exported facings are out of range (bank double doors, inner doors); on-screen tile readout. Topmost walls, posts and pillars rise up to 16 Z to meet a roof eave above them (Britain Public Library hall at 1440-1471,1694-1720 no longer shows sky under its roof); stacked columns are not pinnacles (portico banners hang on full columns); stairwell rails ignore platforms that reach the floor level (bridge-flagged landings at half height); stray roof tiles are dropped only when isolated from every other roof tile (an earlier stricter rule opened holes at ridges). Docks aerial capture not re-checked (capture timed out under memory pressure).
- Wave 3 (Sketchfab via Objaverse, CC-BY, attributed): cutlery/knife/fork/silverware, spoon, tray, bread, kettle, fruit basket, bellows, tubs, iron ore, cauldron, lute, drum, books, spellbook, firewood/logs; wooden table runs use a trestle table repeated along the run (stone/marble tables unchanged). Flat goods scale by length. Rejected after preview: cartoon ham/cheese/fish bucket, textureless banner/rope/chained book, mushrooms on a ground slab, hurdy-gurdy (no UO item). Pinned for the next wave (not yet placed): worn banner, wall torch, iron chandelier, Galway fireplace - they need wall-facing placement.
- Evidence: classic cache rebaked; captures outputs/art-review/defects-a..c; scripts/verify.ps1 passes (0 errors).
- Wave 4 scans + performance (2026-10-28): 17 more CC0/CC-BY Objaverse models pinned (mossy log, rocks, books, bench, inkwell, chests, bottles/carafe, fish, flour sacks, bedroll, cards, rustic fireplace, angel statue, hay pile); heavy scans re-reduced and the heaviest Poly Haven props get decimated copies in Art/Reduced (scripts/reduce-polyhaven.py, source hashes in manifest). Depth-of-field removed (it caused the reported blur) and the frame cost of repeated props cut. Britain placement coverage 86.7% final-quality (scan 13.2%, crafted 59.2%, owner 14.3%); gaps: garbage, mushrooms, wooden/iron fences, lamp posts, banisters, stalagmites, unnamed 2795-2810 records. Evidence: both caches rebaked; scripts/verify.ps1 passes (ArtFidelity 90/0, world items 35/35).
- Wave 5 scans (2026-10-28): scanned fly agaric/amanita mushrooms (mushroom patches per tile), English street lamps and candelabra that keep their lights, an iron wall torch with flame, a worn banner hung flat on the adjacent wall (banners now use the wall-mount planner), a marble angel statue (multi-tile statue drawn once, column statues on a stone pedestal, bird/gargoyle statues excluded), scanned chairs for 2894-2901 with sprite-checked facings, and reviewed benches routed to the scanned bench run. Prop replacements gained per-graphic overrides, variants, clusters, wall anchoring and pedestals. 12 weak candidates rejected (fences, gate, eagles, lions, knight, manure, small lamp, war banner). Britain placement coverage 89.2% final quality (scan 15.7%, crafted 59.2%, owner 14.3%); whole world 90.9% of placements but only 50.3% of 10,709 placed graphics (5,301 open across 1,356 names). Evidence: both caches rebaked; captures outputs/art-review/wave5, wave5b; scripts/verify.ps1 passes (ArtFidelity 93/0, world items 35/35).
- World coverage + memory (2026-10-28): ArtProvenance reports final-quality coverage for Britain, the world, each facet and per placed graphic (Felucca-only graphics now count) and writes outputs/item-catalog/open-work.json; the catalogue page shows it. New tools: scripts/source-candidates.py (bulk CC0/CC-BY candidate search with thumbnail review sheets), reduce-polyhaven.py --split with scripts/blender-split-variants.py (one glTF per variant of a Poly Haven nature set, all variants sharing one texture folder). Wave 6: scanned stumps, stick debris, a pole brazier with fire, spine and pelvis bones (prop replacements gained pitch and sized flames), Poly Haven flower patches by blossom colour (empodium, ursinia, periwinkle, gazania; flowers, orfluer flowers, poppies, campion), FenceBuilder (split-rail wooden fences, wrought-iron railings, stone piers with rails) replacing kit fences, and 5 owner-pack guesses approved after review (most guesses on the sheet did not match and stay open). GPU texture compression for all 3D art (1,619 imports, and now the project import default; owner GUI images stay lossless): Britain stress route GPU memory 3.72 GB to 1.26 GB, textures 3.25 to 0.84 GB, p95 21 to 17.5 ms, startup 6.8 to 3.4 s; the stress test now records GPU memory. Plan for whole facets: docs/WORLD_SCALE_MEMORY_PLAN.md. Coverage: Britain 90.3% of placements, world 93.2% of placements, 51.1% of 10,762 placed graphics. Evidence: both caches rebaked; captures outputs/art-review/wave6, wave6b; scripts/test-streaming.ps1 pass; scripts/verify.ps1 passes (ArtFidelity 93/0, world items 35/35). Gaps: 5,264 open graphics (vines, crystals, stalagmites, hedges, cattails, lilypads, bones, sarcophagi, stone arches, unnamed records), facet-aware streaming and bake not started.
- Stacked fence posts (2026-10-28, owner report at the porch 1491,1622): UO stacks fence pieces (railing pieces at z18/z24, post-only pieces at z30/z36) into tall railings and porch posts. Each piece's post now spans the full source height and is capped only when no fence piece continues above (Reconstruction indexes fence cells), so the stack reads as one pole instead of segments with gaps and caps. Evidence: captures outputs/art-review/porch; ArtFidelity 94/0 (new stacked-post check); scripts/verify.ps1 passes.
- Fence junctions (2026-10-28): fence pieces know their neighbouring fence pieces (Reconstruction indexes fence ids per cell and Z, within 3 Z). Rails run from the tile centre toward each joined neighbour; a post stands at the centre where the art has one or where the run ends or turns, so corners, T-junctions and ends meet without overshooting rails. Iron railings meet at a newel, stone piers carry rails between them; isolated pieces keep the art layout. Remaining: where two meeting runs sit at different source heights (porch side run z18, front run z20), their rails meet with that offset. Evidence: captures outputs/art-review/junctions; ArtFidelity 95/0; scripts/verify.ps1 passes.
- Roof staircases, porch posts, hay on dividers (2026-10-28, owner reports): (1) the stepped dark "notches" across several roofs (slate hall 1449-1473/1702-1716, half-timbered slate house, thatch 1504-1516/1560-1576, shingles 1647-1664/1632-1657, thatch 1663-1680/1543-1552) were interior partition walls raised by the eave rule to each mid-slope roof tile, their timber caps poking through the slope. Walls are now raised only to a roof's eave course (its lowest tiles within 3 Z), so outer walls still meet raised eaves and partitions stay in the attic. (2) Fence posts always stand at the tile centre, so stacked post-only pieces line up with the railing posts below. (3) The top rail of a wooden fence reaches just under the piece height, so hay UO lays on a stall divider (z16 on a z10 h6 divider at 1306,1777 and 1306,1783) rests on a real rail. Evidence: a roof-height scan of Britain (neighbouring tiles off the 3-Z slope rhythm) located the roofs; captures outputs/art-review/roofs-hunt (before) and roofs-fix (after); scripts/verify.ps1 passes (ArtFidelity 95/0).

New CC-BY scans mapped: green crystal clusters (crystal, tall crystal, broken crystals as small clusters), cave flowstone columns, a stalagmite spire group, cattails and bulrushes, lily pads with a flower (lilypad/lilly pad/water lilly families), a skeleton laid flat for bones/skeleton, a tibia for bone; sunken logs use the mossy log scan. Of placed graphics: 1,223 built, 210 kit, 55 your packs, 4,978 guesses, 4,221 original only. Worn items are all "original only" because the avatar's outfit is fixed.

Limits: categories are name/flag heuristics (about 2,100 families remain "other"). Family grouping uses names and nearby IDs only, not picture similarity or mirrored pairs. Vendor stock, loot and multi (house/boat) components are not counted, so wearables and weapons rank near the bottom. The catalogue is proprietary-derived reference material; only the extractor and the viewer page are committed. Converter self-tests gained a PNG CRC check; scripts/verify.ps1 passes.

# Window shutters, Dexsoft props, character sheet and skills â€” 27 October 2026

**Shutters and flower boxes.** UO walls carry no inside/outside information. WindowDressing.gd reads the outdoor side from the source: indoor cells have a roof, upper floor or landing overhead within three cells. Outdoor cells are open to the sky two cells out, with at most a roof eave directly beside the wall. Windows covered on both sides, open on both sides, or facing another wall stay undressed. Of 1,042 Britain windows, 1,012 got an outdoor side and 30 stayed undressed. 946 got MedievalKit (CC0) open shutter pairs, because both neighbouring cells along the wall are solid wall; about half of those also got a flower box. Bridges and masonry slits/arches are never dressed. Dressing is excluded from collision and distant massing. Interior captures (tavern, two-storey house) show none indoors.

That is 20 lightweight modules (doors, shutters, lantern post, chimney, fence, sacks, woodpile, well, handcart, flower box, pots, plaque, basket, rope, crate, barrel, fountain statue) with a manifest of source hash, triangle counts and file SHA-256s. - the picket fence for the 364 wooden-fence statics;
- the sack for flour sacks;
- the kit chimney, with clay pots, as the crown of every grouped chimney stack.

The kit's limewash and dark-oak PBR sets (with ambientCG Plaster002/Wood062 normals) replace the plaster and timber-frame surfaces of every facade. Whole-building kit modules are not tiled: a 2 m roof or stone-wall module is 24kâ€“38k triangles, and UO buildings must keep their source footprints. Dexsoft Medieval Village 1â€“3 and Old Village were extracted locally (owner-licensed, ignored) but not used yet. They are mostly complete prefab houses with baked lighting; their props remain candidates.

Verification: art fidelity 71 checks and all Godot suites pass; the live city, commerce and login tests pass. The live world-item test passed on rerun; its first run hit a transient single-door close timing failure.

# Depth-aware water â€” 27 October 2026

Water.gdshader reads the depth buffer and screen. Beds, piers and banks refract through the shallows and are absorbed with depth (red first). Contact foam forms wherever the surface meets anything, including piers, hulls and banks. Seabeds now shelve away from the shore (1.5 m plus 0.45 m per tile, up to 6 m), so open sea reads deep blue and the coast reads teal. When converted locally (scripts/import-unity-water.py), the owner's Tasharen Water normal map (RGB) and foam mask (alpha) replace the analytic ripples and noise foam; the public build keeps the analytic version. Verified in Forward+ and Compatibility. Same-route profile: p95 18.5 ms, p99 19.8 ms (was 18.4/20.3). All Godot suites pass.

# City defect pass: chimneys, signs, bridges, roofs, walls â€” 27 October 2026

Fixes from owner screenshots, each traced to a source cause and covered by checks (art fidelity 61â†’69):
- **Chimneys:** UO builds a chimney from stacked/adjacent sprite tiles; each was drawn as a full 3.2 m model at its own Z (floating clusters). Connected chimney tiles now form one capped stone stack over the group footprint, rooted at the roof/wall record beneath.
- **Signs:** street signs (0x1297â€“0x129E) now sit on their post top, and plank plaques (0x1F28/0x1F29) lie flat against their wall or stand on two posts. Neither hangs from a shop bracket any more. On click, the server-confirmed text is engraved on both board faces ("the Great Northern Road", "the Northern Bridge").
- **Bridges:** BridgePlan records the deck side of edge parapets, which now sit flush on the deck edge instead of mid-tile over the water. This is a presentation offset; source XYZ is unchanged.
- **Roofs:**
  - "wooden shingles" hip/corner pieces were mapped to slate, which made blue zigzags and broke joins.
  - Gable infill was drawn on hip, valley and storey-step edges. It is now limited to edges open to air; stepped hips close in roof material.
  - Timber/brick gable wedges (24, 25, 193, 194, 85, 86; stacked in >90% of Britain placements) assemble like the plaster ones instead of poking through rakes as teeth.
  - Roofs with no walls beneath default to timber ends.
- **White bands and blocks:** the interior shell's wall caps (wall top up to ceiling) and the masonry over arches were hard-coded to plaster. They now take the wall's or arch's own family.
- **Furniture through walls:** bookcases 2713/2714/2716, chests 3648/3650, drawer 2616 and wardrobe 2641 occur only in northâ€“south runs in upstream placements (e.g. 53/63/51 neighbour pairs along y). They now face east instead of standing sideways through walls.

All Godot suites and the live world-item, city, commerce and login tests pass.

# Realistic plants and cheaper trees (local only) â€” 27 October 2026

`scripts/import-unity-nature.py` (renamed from import-unity-trees.py) now also converts 40 Tree Creator bushes and 51 mesh plants (ferns, tall grass, wildflower groups, lavender, ground cover) from the owner's Unity packs; `LocalPlants.gd` uses them for UO grass, fern, flower and bush records, falling back to the CC0 library. Fixes found while integrating: the BIG Environment Pack meshes are authored Z-up and are now stood upright; plants are emitted as plain mesh nodes because material overrides inside instanced FBX scenes were lost when districts were packed (they rendered as white cards); every converted foliage texture is now mipmapped and VRAM-compressed (all 159 were imported without mipmaps). Tree cost: instanced vegetation is batched per 16 m cell so mesh LOD applies per cell, and `mesh_lod/lod_change/threshold_pixels=2.0`. Same Forward+ route versus the first local-tree profile: p95 25.4â†’18.4 ms, p99 â†’20.3 ms, startup 2.8 s. # Rooted vegetation and flower-bed planters â€” 26 October 2026

Owner-reported flying trees and flowers. 90 "FlowerBed" pieces were classified as flowers (name match), so the 1 m raised beds were missing and the flowers/trees planted on them floated; they are now stone-curbed planters with soil at source height. UO stores one height per tile while the presented terrain interpolates between tiles, so vegetation at raw tile height floated or sank on slopes; trees, flowers, grass, bushes, mushrooms, rocks and logs now root on the higher of the rendered terrain at the tile centre and any planter/floor top beneath them, as a presentation offset (source XYZ unchanged; cases more than 2.5 m away keep their source height). 442 Godot checks (+5), parity, GPU and native city checks pass.

# Roof ends follow their walls â€” 26 October 2026

Owner-reported white blocks and "sails" over brick and timber buildings: every roof end (and source gable piece) was drawn in plaster regardless of the building, which became glaring once plaster was brightened. Each roof now takes the dominant family of the walls beneath it within three cells (brick, stone, timber or plaster), and source gables use their own family. 437 Godot checks (+1), parity, GPU and native city checks pass. Still open from the same screenshots: crude placeholder statues and an unidentified white board-on-posts prop near Britain's west bank.

# Stair facing from tiledata â€” 26 October 2026

Owner-reported crossing staircases: stair direction was inferred only from a same-graphic neighbour one step higher and otherwise defaulted to "rises north", so 316 of 926 Britain stair tiles (flight ends, landings, wide stairs, daises) faced the wrong way and intersected their flights. UO stair graphics carry their facing in tiledata (StairBack = east, StairRight = west, both = south, neither = north), which agreed with every unambiguous neighbour inference; it is now authoritative for "stairs" graphics, while low "step" pieces keep inference. 434 Godot checks (new four-direction test), parity, GPU and native city checks pass.

# Sea, Tudor plaster, stone merlons and water â€” 26 October 2026

Owner-reported defects fixed through shared rules. (1) 95,546 open-sea land tiles (map.mul "water", ~18% of the city dataset) had no water presentation and rendered as grass; terrain now presents them as a 1.5 m bed under a merged sea surface, the shoreline field includes them, and the ground shader treats them as silt. (2) Flicker: 935 river cells had a water plane exactly at terrain height, and plank/dock decks sat coplanar with the ground; sea quads skip cells that already have water objects and terrain under ground-level floors drops 6 cm (presentation only; movement uses source data). (3) Plaster houses follow owner-supplied York references: close-studded near-black oak (studs every 0.5 m, sill rail, heavy bressumer, run-end posts, braces) proud of bright white rough-cast using the photoscanned plaster relief, and leaded diamond-pane windows; studs never cross source openings. (4) Stone/brick wall tops (castle and tower merlons, parapets) were the village plaster/timber kit wall; they are now solid masonry with a coping. (5) Water shading is a continuous world-space normal (directional swells plus smooth analytic ripples, distance-faded), deep/shallow colour from shoreline depth and shore foam; the old tangent-space normal made each tile visibly different.

The stream bake now fails (no stamps, no index) when any prepared resource cannot be written â€” a running client keeps its districts open and previously caused stale districts to be stamped current. Evidence: 428 Godot checks (+5 new), prepared parity, GPU checks in both renderers, native city traversal and world-object checks pass; owner captures inspected locally. Remaining: trees (stylized library), stairs with crossing flights, roof/gable shapes and the converter test block.

# Free visual upgrade (step 2) â€” 26 October 2026

Turf now uses two pinned Poly Haven CC0 photoscans (`leafy_grass` close detail, `aerial_grass_rock` 15 m colour field) instead of a procedural green ramp. Lighting uses the AgX tonemapper, a light contrast/saturation grade and SSAO that also affects lit areas. Facade plaster takes tonal variation from its photoscan, varies between cream/ochre/grey lime washes by neighbourhood, and gains base damp, staining and streaks recovered from each piece's base/height (also under MultiMesh instancing); timber is darker, deeper-grained and its framing now projects from the infill. The bake now refuses to run when a pinned surface is not imported (a previous bake had silently stamped blank materials as current).

Tried and rejected: SDFGI bounce light plus volumetric haze made the streamed city GPU-bound (median ~30 ms, GPU p99 49 ms on the profile route); lower-cost SDFGI settings were still unstable while moving, so both are disabled. Measured back to back on the same route: GPU p99 20.6 â†’ 21.6 ms, frame p99 22.5 â†’ 22.5 ms.

Honest status: ground and timber read clearly better; plaster walls still look smooth at street distance, and trees/props/building geometry remain the stylized low-poly library and simple box construction. Those are the dominant remaining "toy" cues and need better models (owner-approved art kit decision), not shader work.

District loading no longer produces 100 ms+ frames on the profiled walking route: worst frame 326 â†’ 40 ms (OpenGL) and 306 â†’ 80 ms (Forward+), frames over 100 ms 22/24 â†’ 0, p99 32.1 â†’ 18.7 ms / 29.3 â†’ 21.3 ms, startup ~1 s faster. Retained memory +~40 MiB; a few 60â€“80 ms Forward+ frames remain. See STREAMING_AND_MINIMAP.md.

# Bridges, wall attachments and contact corrections â€” 26 October 2026

Shared `BridgePlan.gd` recognizes plank decks over water/riverbed, bridge-flagged stone piers and parapets from source relationships. Window art standing on bridge piers (graphic 94, 12 placements) now renders as dressed-stone towers with the source's barred slit instead of plaster walls with timber casements; low stone walls along decks render as stone parapets with a coping. Explicitly plaster/wooden construction beside a bridge is untouched, and the same graphic away from bridges is not reinterpreted. Fourteen window/battlement graphics whose tiledata names are generic ("window", "arrow loop", "battlement") now carry inspected art facts (stone/brick family, slit/arched opening) in `reviewed-catalog.json`; the castle curtain walls lost their plaster window slabs.

"Wall torch", "wall sconce" and "wall map" names were classified as walls (152 placements rendered as plaster panels). They are now wall-hung props: each is hung on the face of the adjacent west/north source wall as a recorded presentation offset; source XYZ is unchanged and unsupported cases stay put. Contact now recognizes exact/surface-flag supports, procedural table/counter tops (0.82/1.0 m rather than native 1.2 m), legacy rugs and same-graphic barrel stacks; duplicate beds stacked above identical beds are context-suppressed like benches/tables. The bridge/bank audit no longer reports water statics over riverbed as bank gaps â€” all 301 earlier candidates were that case.

The refreshed context-aware audit inspects 1,409 reviewed placements: 1,286 supported, 103 presentation contact adjustments, 13 context-suppressed duplicates and **7 unsupported candidates** (was 53): two wine bottles above bar tops (gaps 3/5), a bottle on cards on a table, an elevated table and cards over a rug, one bed and one bench with no source support. Bridge/bank candidates fell from 301 to 1. Native headless city traversal (62 accepted steps, reconciliation, minimap, F6) and native world-object checks pass after the change.

During this session the main server was restarted twice (00:52 and 00:57) by a separate pwsh start-server.ps1 (Codex), which drops every client; a young character's death moving its ghost to the nearest healer is ModernUO's intended young-player behaviour. A live death test needs [PreviewDie, installed on the next server restart.
